<p>I want to come back to the motivation in the speech of the hon member for Toekomsrus and the fact that they have not replied to me on one single He I supposedly told here. I am actually grateful that they did not give the floor to speakers who are too scared to agree with me, because now I can have a more tranquil journey back to Ceres to address a public meeting there this evening. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I now put the question. Those in favour say &#x201C;aye&#x201D;&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from>*<person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Aye!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>Cowards!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

<p>The hon member for Border must withdraw that word.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>I withdraw it, Sir.</p>

<p>Question put: That all the words after &#x201C;That&#x201D; stand part of the Question,</p>

<p>Upon which the House divided.</p>

<p>As fewer than fifteen members (viz T R George, C B Herandien, F G Herwels, S Hoosen, C J Kippen, P A S Mopp, P J Muller, J A Rabie and C R Redcliffe) appeared on one side,</p>

<p>Question declared negatived and the words omitted.</p>

<p>Substitution of the words proposed by Mr I Richards put,</p>

<p>Upon which the House divided.</p>

<p>As fewer than fifteen members (viz T R George, C B Herandien, F G Herwels, S Hoosen, C J Kippen, P A S Mopp, P J Muller, J A Rabie and C R Redcliffe) appeared on one side,</p>

<p>Substitution of the words declared agreed to.</p>

<p>Main Question, as amended, accordingly agreed to, viz: That the House expresses its full confidence in the leadership, policy directions and principles of the Labour Party of South Africa and further urgently calls upon the Cabinet to accelerate the reform process in order that all citizens of South Africa will be included in the decision-making process at all levels and to clearly define these reforms and to bring them about by way of legislation.</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#adjournment_of_house">

<heading>ADJOURNMENT OF HOUSE</heading>

<summary>(Motion)</summary>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I move:</p>

<block name="quote">That the House do now adjourn.</block>

<p>Agreed to.</p>

</speech>

<adjournment>

<p>The House adjourned at <recordedTime time="1988-02-09T17:21:00">17h21.</recordedTime></p>

</adjournment>

</debateSection>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#proceedings_of_the_house_of_delegates">

<heading><span class="col_379-380" refersTo="page_0212"/><i>PROCEEDINGS OF THE HOUSE OF DELEGATES</i></heading>

<prayers>

<narrative>Prayers&#x2014;<recordedTime time="1988-02-09T14:15:00">14h15.</recordedTime></narrative>

</prayers>

<debateSection name="#referral_of_draft_bill_and_memorandum_to_standing_committee_on_private_members_draft_bills">

<heading>REFERRAL OF DRAFT BILL AND MEMORANDUM TO STANDING COMMITTEE ON PRIVATE MEMBERS&#x2019; DRAFT BILLS</heading>

<p>The ACTING SPEAKER announced that in terms of Rule 23 (4) he had referred the following draft Bill which had been submitted to him, together with the memorandum thereon, to the Standing Committee on Private Members&#x2019; Draft Bills:</p>

<block name="quote"><i>Freedom of Farming Bill,</i> submitted by Mr J V Iyman.</block>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#debate_on_motion_of_no_confidence_in_the_ministers_council">

<heading>DEBATE ON MOTION OF NO CONFIDENCE IN THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</heading>

<summary>(Resumed)</summary>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, to an extent a development occurred this morning which probably has made part of this Motion superfluous. This morning the House of Delegates namely passed a resounding vote of no confidence in the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and the leader of the National Peoples Party.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Your elation will be short-lived!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am not elated, I am merely reporting facts as they occur.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I at this stage, because it is possible that I may exceed the half-hour time limit, crave the indulgence of the Chair, to accord me the one-hour privilege in terms of Standing Order 137(l)(b)? I am indebted to the Chair. Unfortunately, during the recess the hon member Mr Gopie Munsook passed away. As a consequence a vacancy was created in this House which had to be filled in terms of Section 43(l)(c) of the Constitution Act of the Republic of South Africa.</p>

<p>After due notice a senior official of the Department of Home Affairs, who acted as returning officer, conducted the election. There were two candidates. One candidate was present in the ante-chamber to the Chamber in which the election was actually conducted and I must say, he cut a very handsome figure. He was trim, well-dressed and very good-looking. He was here for several days. So confident, obviously, was his leader that he would in fact be elected that Mr Panday was brought from Durban&#x2014;really, I suspect, to present himself in this august chamber for admission. He was the candidate favoured by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

<p>Solidarity Party put up another candidate, a gentleman by the name of Mr Abul Samud Razak. When I went into the voting hall I took it for granted that because of the great majority the NPP had, their candidate&#x2014;after all, they normally do as their leader says in this Chamber&#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Don&#x2019;t be deceitful. Every one had a democratic right to vote.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I took it for granted that their candidate would be elected. I do not know what these hon members are getting so excited about. [Interjections.] Some people are becoming very hot under the collar and I would like to advise them that unlike our offices, the air-conditioning here is in fact functioning and so they need not become excited.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Why are you not truthful sometimes?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>So Mr Razak was elected, and what was the result of that? The result was that there had to be a person from the NPP or from among the ranks of the Independents who claimed to align themselves with the NPP and who did not have enough confidence in the judgement of their own leader.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Oh, you are talking rubbish now!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>They then supported the Solidarity Party in electing Mr Razak [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Everyone had a democratic right to vote for whom they wanted.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I quite agree, Sir. [Interjections.] Those hon members exercised <span class="col_381-382" refersTo="page_0213"/>their democratic right because that voting took place in secret. I believe that if the voting in this chamber took place in secret, we would probably see different results.</p>

<p>What did we observe last year? With regard to the Marketing Amendment Bill the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council said to me directly: &#x201C;We shall combine and reject this Bill unless the Government does certain things.&#x201D; What happened? One of his other colleagues in the Cabinet came and spoke to his party and the very next day they did an about-face. They supported that particular Bill.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>He accepted our condition; that is something you did not know.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>The Remuneration of Town Clerks Amendment Bill was opposed unanimously in the Standing Committee and the hon member Mr Abram advanced a very cogent and rational criticism in the standing committee. What happened when it came before this House? Obviously the hon the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning had leaned on the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, and lo and behold, that opposition fell away. Not even the trumpet was required to cause that particular little wall to fall. That is the kind of thing we have had because those votes were taken in this Chamber in open view.</p>

<p>This morning the voting took place in secret and the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council will never know who defied his instructions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rampersadh">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">H RAMPERSADH</person>:</from>

<p>Of course we know!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>If he had commanded the confidence of his own supporters the result would have been different. [Interjections.] I am very surprised that the hon member for Newholme claims to know who voted how.</p>

<p>I am certain that the senior official from the Department of Home Affairs who conducted the election would not have revealed it, even if he knew. However, there was no way for even the returning officer to know. I normally have great respect for everything that the hon member says, but today he could not possibly mean what he is saying. If he does know &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rampersadh">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">H RAMPERSADH</person>:</from>

<p>I respect you from YOSA settlement days when you were the chairman of that institution.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member disarms me with that comment.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, it is not only the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council in whom we say we have no confidence. We say that we have no confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council as a whole. We also say that we have no confidence in what the Ministers&#x2019; Council as a whole is doing. I will give the reason why we have no confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council as a whole outright.</p>

<p>Let us take the Group Areas Act. In the last 40 years there has been nothing as vile, evil and wicked as this instrument of legalised robbery which is the Group Areas Act. We have said before, I have said this outside and I repeat it here: The worst thing the Group Areas Act did was to murder settled communities. Even the most unlearned person will know that a community is something which takes years and years, sometimes generations, to mature. A neighbourhood is merely a collection of people living in houses in a particular geographical area, but it takes a very long time for a community and a community spirit to develop. A community is bound together by silken threads, but those silken threads can be stronger than the strongest tempered steel. What was perpetrated in terms of the Group Areas Act was that these communities were destroyed wholesale. Cato Manor and District Six were destroyed wholesale. Those people who were evicted from there became displaced persons.</p>

<p>Incidently, the expression &#x201C;displaced person&#x201D; these days has another meaning entirely. It no longer evokes the kind of sympathy and compassion to which a displaced person is entitled. &#x201C;Displaced person&#x201D; has become an excuse for skullduggery and thievery. Those displaced persons were resettled. Chatsworth was such a resettlement area. This is the expression that the Department of Community Destruction used when they built new houses and said to people: &#x201C;Either you take that house, or you will be criminally prosecuted for living in this house which your grandfather built.&#x201D; I have had correspondence with the Department of Community Destruction in that respect, in which I pointed out to them that they want to make criminals out of decent, law-abiding, loyal South Africans. That is how disgusting that department&#x2019;s behaviour was. That is how disgraceful the crimes which were perpetrated in terms of the Group Areas Act have been.</p>

<p><span class="col_383-384" refersTo="page_0214"/>I suppose most hon members will receive a letter from somebody called Wendy Parkhouse, who is the editor of the <i>Reader&#x2019;s Digest.</i> I also received such a letter, in which she is asking members of Parliament to raise their voices in protest against the Group Areas Act.</p>

<p>I wrote to her and pointed out to her that perhaps she was not even warned when we started the protest, because if she had been following events in South Africa she would have known that we have been raising our voices in protest against this evil for a very long time.</p>

<p>What does the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council do without any protest from his colleagues in the Ministers&#x2019; Council? He says that if one does not want the Group Areas Act in one&#x2019;s area, one has to tell him and he will ask the hon the State President to repeal it. We do not want the Group Areas Act. The community does not want the Group Areas Act. I do not know who goes and tells him things in secret. He is constantly claiming that people come to him and tell him things in secret. Let any member of the community say publicly that he support the Group Areas Act and wants it to be retained. To the hon the members of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and those who support them, whether they joined them last year, in 1981 or last week, I want to say that they are equally guilty here because they raised no protest at the expression of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. I am not going to spare anybody because we are now dealing with matters which vitally affect the community.</p>

<p>I want to say here and now that we were told yesterday that a certain two hon MPs entered into &#x201C;a gentleman&#x2019;s agreement&#x201D; that one area would be declared a Coloured area and the other declared an Indian area. I do not think any gentleman worthy of the name would ever enter into such an agreement because that is asking for group areas. How can anyone stand up in this House or in any of the other Houses and say that he opposes the Group Areas Act&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member whether there is any provision for mixed residential areas in the Group Areas Act presently?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>We do not want provision for mixed residential areas. We want an equal situation for every South African, be he Black, White or so-called &#x201C;Indian&#x201D;. I say: &#x201C;so-called Indian&#x201D; because I do not think that Indians are permitted to own land in South Africa in terms of their own country&#x2019;s currency regulations. I do not think India will allow her nationals to buy land in South Africa. In political terms we are not Indians, but South Africans.</p>

<p>This is where the Afrikaner is far more intelligent and sensible than we are. They do not call themselves Dutchmen or French. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>They are more intelligent than you are, not more intelligent than us.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>They are far more intelligent than that hon member. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>The least educated Afrikaner is capable of understanding English words better than the hon the Minister of the Budget. They call themselves Suid-Afrikaners. Why can we not say that we are &#x201C;Suid-Afrikaners?&#x201D; Actually I said this in my first speech in the President&#x2019;s Council. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>You would have loved to have been called Harry!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>That may be so if you are Tom and also Dick. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I said in the President&#x2019;s Council: &#x201C;Ek is &#x2019;n Suid-Afrikaner met &#x2019;n Indi&#x00EB;r-pappa.&#x201D; [Interjections.] What do we get from the Ministers&#x2019; Council? We get some words that the Group Areas Act must go, but the Group Areas Act is still applied.</p>

<p>After a great deal of to-ing and fro-ing and a great deal of gurgitation and regurgitation, the President&#x2019;s Council produced a certain report.</p>

<p>That report was a bit of a problem. A member of the NPP signed that report and he has claimed publicly that he was instructed to sign it by his leader, the hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. Of course that has been denied by the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and I understand that that particular contretemps has not yet been settled. Nevertheless the official record shows that that report was supported by at least one member of the NPP.</p>

<p>What does the hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council himself say? He is moving like those Studebakers we used to have in 1942. The hon <span class="col_385-386" refersTo="page_0215"/>member Mr Nowbath will remember his son referring to that as a which-way car, because the front and the rear look almost identical excepting for the headlamps. [Interjections.] The reaction of the Chairman&#x2014;and unfortunately his wizardry failed him terribly this morning&#x2014;was a which way reaction. In their heart of hearts each hon member of the Ministers&#x2019; Council must know that that report is a wishy-washy thing. That report does not make the kind of recommendations that are necessary and they ought to have said so, but they didn&#x2019;t.</p>

<p>The reason for that is obvious; they cannot risk offending their master. We all know what happened to the leader of the Labour Party when he dared to offend his master. We all know what happened to him.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Talk about what the hon the State President said to you in this House on 20 August.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, these people dare not take a similar risk. We hear talk about improving the Group Areas Act, but that Act cannot be improved. It is so filthy that you can never hope to improve it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Do you hope to kill it by talking about it?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, I hope by talking to try to enter the hearts and minds of people and I hope that even at this late stage the members on that side and to my left are not beyond redemption. The hon member for Cavendish said yesterday that there was a divine spark in all of us; let us hope that is so. Let us hope that there is still that innate decency and innate integrity in all people which can be kindled and rekindled as a result of words. If words fail completely to persuade or to convince people then there is no need for any Parliament in any country. One could then simply have a dictatorship and let the dictator rule by decree.</p>

<p>As I was saying, Mr Chairman, it is impossible to improve the Act. That Act must go! I will not take it beyond that, because there is a motion on the Order Paper dealing with the request that the Group Areas Act be repealed forthwith, and once before the Chairman ruled that if a motion is on the Order Paper one may not anticipate the discussion of that motion.</p>

<p>I now want to deal with another NPP member.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member take another question? Can the hon member indicate to us whether his coalition partners are in favour of repealing the Group Areas Act? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I have no coalition partners. We are friendly with everyone and when any hon members are prepared to share their views with us, and we agree with each other, then of course we can act in concert. The hon member Mr Abram will understand that perfectly.</p>

<p>A member of the NPP in the President&#x2019;s Council&#x2014;poor man; I sympathise with him&#x2014;signed a report, and astoundingly he told the newspapers that he signed the report without even knowing what it contained.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>What about the Kannemeyer Report?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I did not sign the Kannemeyer Report. [Interjections.] If the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council wants to use his influence with his pals across the road to elevate me to the position of Mr Justice Kannemeyer, I would consider signing a report.</p>

<p>The Ministers&#x2019; Council administers own affairs. Own affairs is apartheid. It separates the function of government. It is an administrative nightmare.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Is this not an apartheid Chamber?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I do not know whether the hon the Minister of Education in the House of Assembly heard us say this but I certainly put it to the Minister of Finance when he was before this House in another Chamber that the triplication and quadruplication of services is a terrible waste of taxpayers&#x2019; money. I do not know whether the hon gentleman from the House of Assembly heard that or whether he thought it up himself, but he made a first class statement before his own party in public. It is a terrible waste and at a time when the hon the State President&#x2014;with respect, correctly&#x2014;has set out measures to bring about economic improvement and an enhancement of the economic status of all the people in this country, a tremendous amount of money is still being wasted on own affairs.</p>

<p>But of course it is there and in terms of that what <span class="col_387-388" refersTo="page_0216"/>is being done regarding the second access road to Chatsworth? The people of Chatsworth have been clamouring for 22 years. The former South Africa Indian Council failed to alleviate the plight suffered by the people of Chatsworth. In the House of Delegates the Minister of Transport at that time, the hon Mr Schoeman, actually stated that he would give instructions for the cheaper and more direct route to be followed. After that a few people&#x2014;a very small number of White people&#x2014;objected, and there the matter stands. The White people who objected&#x2014;the few White people&#x2014;obviously have more influence with the Government than the entire Ministers&#x2019; Council&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>And the entire House of Delegates.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026; and the entire House of Delegates. Exactly. I am indebted to the hon member because the Ministers&#x2019; Council does not adequately put forward the viewpoint of the House of Delegates to the Cabinet. Admittedly, the hon gentleman who serves in the Cabinet will tell us that Cabinet discussions are confidential. Some members of his caucus get to know the day after the Cabinet meeting what the Cabinet decided, but that is a different matter. If anyone challenges me on that I will say &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: I do want to object that the hon member for Reservoir Hills states that members of the caucus know what goes on in Cabinet discussions. It is a serious reflection on me as a member. It is an indictment and I want it placed on record that I will be pursuing this matter according to the rules of this House. No member of the caucus is given Cabinet secrets by me. I say this under oath in this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I put it to the hon member for Reservoir Hills that that statement is highly irregular. It is policy that Cabinet matters are strictly confidential and classified. For an hon member to state that a Cabinet Minister divulges information, be it to members of the Cabinet, the caucus or anyone else, is a serious indictment. I would therefore request the hon member for Reservoir Hills to withdraw and apologise to the hon the Minister.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member for Rylands, when he was sitting in the opposition benches, was, like the rest of us, aware of information emanating from the caucus of the NPP. They knew, long before the ministerial representatives&#x2019; appointments were to be created, that that was coming, and that the Cabinet had made a decision to that effect.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will the hon member for Reservoir Hills withdraw the statement and apologise to the hon the Minister.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I first ask you for a ruling: Did I cast any direct reflection upon the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Yes. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>My words were, and I chose my words carefully &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will the hon member for Reservoir Hills withdraw that statement and apologise to the hon the Minister.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I withdraw the statement and I apologise because I need to continue&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Reservoir Hills may resume his speech.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Thank you, Mr Chairman.</p>

<p>I just want to make one thing clear. What I said was that the caucus gets to know&#x2014;I did not say from whom they get to know; they could get to know from anybody, from any official or anything. However, I am aware of the Chairman&#x2019;s ruling.</p>

<p>Now, why has nothing been achieved with regard to the second access road? The promise was made by the hon the Minister of Transport nearly two years ago. Nothing has been finalised.</p>

<p>I wish to deal with health matters. It is notorious that while the Addington Hospital has a CAT scanner machine&#x2014;apparently it has a very long name, but it is known as a CAT scanner&#x2014;the CAT at Addington is not over-used. King Edward Hospital does not have one; Wentworth has one; the R K Khan Hospital, which serves a community of almost 450 000 if you take people from other parts of Durban, does not have one.</p>

<p><span class="col_389-390" refersTo="page_0217"/>We may be told that insufficient funds were available.</p>

<p>Phoenix consists of more than 150 000 people. A hospital has been promised in respect of Phoenix for many years. That hospital has not got off the ground. We were told quite categorically by the then Minister of Health that it was because of lack of funds that that hospital could not get off the ground. If that is true, where did the funds come from for the Odeon cinema to be purchased and for the purchase price to be paid within 32 days of the negotiations for the purchase of that cinema having commenced?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>That is not a House of Delegates record.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Where did the money come from for the supermarket site in Chatsworth to be purchased?</p>

<p>That was public money. Here we have a situation where people are desperate and need a proper hospital facility in Phoenix. However, we are told that there is no money. Do hon members want to know why? Except perhaps for the awarding of the building contract, there is nothing in it for anyone. As regards the Checkers supermarket site, more information regarding that transaction will be divulged in due course. Everyone knows all about the Checkers supermarket site at Phoenix. Let us examine the connection there. Mr Dina Pillay I am told&#x2014;I did not see the transaction&#x2014;has contributed R20 000 to the National People&#x2019;s Party for the Tongaat election campaign.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like to ask a question of the hon member and I would also like to ask for your ruling. As we commenced yesterday, you gave a ruling in respect of the following privilege. I want to appeal to the Chair that that privilege be used by people who will be able to substantiate statements outside this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>He is wasting my time. I have not got&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Is the hon member prepared to make this statement outside this House when we have adjourned?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I do not want to waste time on people like this.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Don&#x2019;t squirm.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I did not say that this hon gentleman stole the money from Mr Dina Pillay. I said &#x2026; [Interjections.] &#x2026; be quiet! [Interjections.] Yes, Mr Dina Pillay is connected, has had transactions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>No, no.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I know it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You are misleading this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I will go on now. I will deal with certain childish statements made in the newspapers recently. They have not been denied as far as I am aware. I spoke to the journalist concerned today, who says that that report has not been denied. The fact is that instructions were given to the hon members of the Ministers&#x2019; Council not to speak to me because the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council contends that I write nasty letters to our hon Ministers and nice letters to White Ministers. This is naked racism, Mr Chairman. In point of fact, I have written some letters to the hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council which were unpleasant &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>And quite astute.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I wrote one letter to one hon Minister which was contentious, but none of the other hon Ministers can ever say that I have ever written an unpleasant letter to them. Fortunately his own hon Ministers have more intelligence than to indulge in such childishness. They don&#x2019;t play &#x201C;no speaks&#x201D; and cut it out. At least their integrity remains.</p>

<p>I am not going to deal <i>in extensio</i> with the section 205 inquiry, but the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has claimed that he instigated my appearance in court. Undoubtedly this will be a subject of discussion next Monday when the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council moves his particular motion. However, I have here a statement which I have issued. There is not enough time to read it into the record, but I shall issue copies of the statement. However, it is important that I state in this House that I shall give my information to a commission of inquiry or in a court of law. I was commanded to go to the <span class="col_391-392" refersTo="page_0218"/>court of law, which I did. Apparently a request had been made to the senior public prosecutor that his hearing should be in private. Section 205 gives a magistrate the discretion and it is in fact permissive. It reads: &#x201C;He may hold a hearing in private.&#x201D; I, however, insisted that the hearing be in public because I had nothing to be afraid of. I had nothing to hide except a name. Now, who made a public statement detailing all the matters outside the privilege of Parliament &#x2026; ?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>No, I do not have the time.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You did not repeat that in court!</p>

<p>[Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Reservoir Hills may continue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Thank you, Mr Chairman. The only thing I did not divulge in public was the name of the person concerned, and the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council knows the reason why I did not divulge that name. He has repeatedly asked me for the information, and I wrote to him. He has received my letter. He has in fact acknowledged my letter. I told him that the person concerned was very frightened. He gave as a reason the fact that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council was a Cabinet Minister and that the Government would do everything to support him because he supports the Government.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>That informant does not exist! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>He was afraid. Some other reasons are given by people.</p>

<p>One man who has information refuses to allow his name to be used or to provide an affidavit because, he has told me, a former member of the Southern Durban Local Affairs Committee by the name of A Rajbansi was accused by Mr C G Pillay of irregular conduct while he was a member of that Local Affairs Committee.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: I want to state on record that the person who complained about me to the Town Clerk was a Mr B G Pillay and not Mr C G Pillay. I would like that to be recorded. I am speaking under oath in Parliament. It was not Mr C G Pillay. It was Mr B G Pillay.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I do not know what happened. I am giving the reasons as to why this man is afraid.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Your informant does not exist. He is locked in a post box.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>That man says that as a result of the inquiry the gentleman concerned, Mr A Rajbansi, was barred from becoming a member of any local authority in Natal.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>A political vendetta!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Shortly after that Mr C G Pillay was murdered in his own home. Whether or not that man has any justification for being so, he is frightened. I think he has the wrong end of the stick.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: I need the protection of the Chair. I personally believe, Mr Chairman, that the suggestion in the ruling you gave yesterday in respect of using Parliamentary privilege for the purpose for which Parliament passes legislation, is being abused. I stated in my request to you to give a ruling that hon members who make statements in this House must be prepared to substantiate them outside without any reference to informants having informed them. I think the privilege of this House is being abused by the hon member for Reservoir Hills. He is going to extremes. He keeps on saying: &#x2018;Informant, informant, informant&#x2019;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is deliberately getting up all the time in an attempt to waste my time and therefore I want to proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>You are misleading the House! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I want to deal now with what happened in Tongaat.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I <span class="col_393-394" refersTo="page_0219"/>would request the hon member for Reservoir Hills kindly to take note of what I stated yesterday in regard to any addresses that hon members may make, namely that if there are any allegations these should be made by way of substantive motions. The rules of Parliament allow for that. Hon members should not, however, be vague in their presentations. I would appeal to the hon member to kindly observe the appeal I made yesterday at the commencement of this debate.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>With respect, Mr Chairman, I did not volunteer that information. I have given it as a result of repeated demands by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council that I should name my sources. I am explaining why I could not name my sources.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point or order: I did not make any demands for any persons&#x2019; names to be mentioned. I made no such demands. The hon member told a lie in court.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council must withdraw the word &#x201C;lie&#x201D;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>And apologise.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I will withdraw the word, but I need some clarification. Yesterday you clarified the point that a person may not call someone a liar on the basis of what he says in this House. You gave a ruling, Mr Chairman, and I noted that ruling very carefully. <i>I am</i> referring to a statement the hon member made outside this House. I will apologise and abide by the ruling of the Chair, but I very clearly remember that you have laid down a directive.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, these frivolous interjections are taking up the hon member&#x2019;s time and I think he should be permitted to speak.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I want to move fairly fast now, and I appeal to the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council not to be obstructive and let me finish what I want to say.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>All right, but speak the truth.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>That gentleman, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, has alleged that a member of this hon House was kidnapped and held in prison against his will. That is a criminal offence. He has alleged that someone offered a bribe of R10 000 to a member of Parliament to induce him to switch sides. That is a criminal offence in terms of the Prevention of Corruption Act. He has also alleged that someone offered what amounts to a bribe of a &#x201C;fantastic sum of money&#x201D;&#x2014;these were his very words&#x2014;in connection with Villa Liza, that if any person were to declare or help to declare Villa Liza &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Not Villa Liza; Windmill Park.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Well, I will quote from Hansard. The memory of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is somewhat faulty. Sometimes his memory gets faulty within half an hour. I will read from Hansard, 22 May 1987, col 226. The Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council said, and I quote:</p>

<block name="quote">Regarding the East Rand we have identified four areas. We are interested in Villa Liza. The advertisement did not indicate the boundary on which we had agreed with the Department of Constitutional Planning and Development. Why is it that somebody is blocking every other area we are examining? Who is doing this?<br/>They are eating Villa Liza like a lollipop. They are going beserk&#x2014;Villa Liza! Villa Liza! Mona Lisa! Nothing but Villa Liza! The chairman of the management committee of Boksburg told me that fantastic commissions are being offered to people just to have it declared Indian.</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Villa Liza; not Windmill Park.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I continue:</p>

<block name="quote">We have decided to buy Villa Liza.</block>

<p>Mr Chairman, I cannot say that the man is denying &#x2026; Yes, indeed, he is denying that which &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: I corrected that statement subsequently.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council changed his statement. Everyone knows that certain people&#x2014;I cannot point to any hon member in this House&#x2014;trim their sails to try and reap the winds that they have to face. Certain people do that very regularly. Everyone in this House knows who those people or that person is.</p>

<p><span class="col_395-396" refersTo="page_0220"/>A Cabinet member alleges that serious crimes have been committed. Even an ordinary citizen, if he knows about those crimes and does not report it to the appropriate authority, is committing an offence himself. When a member of the hon the State President&#x2019;s Cabinet knows that these crimes were committed&#x2014;whether it was in connection with Villa Lisa or Windmill Park is immaterial, it is still a crime&#x2014;what does one make of that Cabinet Minister? Is he worthy to sit on these benches? Is he worthy to be Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council? The answer is obvious.</p>

<p>What happened in the Tongaat election? We have a situation where certainly the number of persons who were identified as voters hold what is possibly a world record. We have sworn affidavits. Obviously the hon the State President has already acknowledged the receipt of the affidavits which were submitted. One of these affidavits relates to a prot&#x00E9;g&#x00E9; of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. His name is A K Mohamed, known as Mike Mohamed, who was elevated pretty fast to the position of undersecretary in the Department of Education in charge of staffing matters. That man has been playing havoc, moving teachers, offering promotions to teachers if they support the National Peoples Party.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: Sometime last year you gave a ruling that hon members should not mention the names of civil servants who are not here to defend themselves. The hon member is now ignoring your ruling. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, there was no such ruling. The hon member is talking absolute nonsense.</p>

<p>Mr A K Mohamed was put into that position by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. He has now become the lackey of the NPP. What is worse, is that it is alleged in this affidavit, and I quote:</p>

<block name="quote">The said Mr Mohamed instructed me that when I was called into the polling station I would be given a form to sign. He instructed me that I should be careful to sign the name &#x201C;K Naicker.&#x201D; He repeated this instruction several times, both in the car while we were travelling and while we were on the verandah of the magistrate&#x2019;s office where the voting was to take place. When I went in I was given a form which I signed as directed by Mr Mike Mohamed and I thereafter cast a vote for and on behalf of Mr Naicker.</block>

<p>I wrote to the hon the State President and to the hon the Minister of Justice and the hon the Minister of Law and Order. I particularly addressed the hon the Minister of Justice as the Minister bearing overall responsibility for the administration of justice. While he cannot endeavour to influence our judges and our magistrates, he can&#x2014;and in this case it is respectfully submitted that he should&#x2014;influence the relevant attorney-general or public prosecutor to request the judicial officer to grant immunity from prosecution to those persons who were used, manoeuvred or manipulated by persons in positions of authority to commit acts that amount to serious criminal offences, subject of course to the testimony being accepted as truthful and satisfactory.</p>

<p>I also said in my letter, and I quote:</p>

<block name="quote">The real culprits, namely those who plan and organise the falsity in voting, must be identified and those who are manipulated should&#x2014; this I submit to the State&#x2014;not be made in American parlance &#x201C;a fall guy.&#x201D; I have little doubt that the hon the State President and the hon the Ministers who are addressed will agree that it is essential that a special team from headquarters be assigned with specific instructions not to protect or cover up for any person, no matter how exalted his status and no matter what his political leaning is. In effect I am saying to the hon the State President that just because someone gives his Government some support, he should not be allowed to be whitewashed, whoever he may be.<br/>It is a very serious state of affairs which does not give Denmark but the House of Delegates a very rotten atmosphere.</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>No, I do not have enough time.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>That is an escape route!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>In regard to the Villa Liza controversy I took the trouble of writing to the hon the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning, asking him to see to it that no corruption takes place. I also asked him to see to it that land was made available for people. I <span class="col_397-398" refersTo="page_0221"/>said that I did not want any group areas, but certain members of my group as defined by the Group Areas Act must have land, as must the Black people, the so-called Coloured people and the White people. However, there is no reason whatsoever why in terms of the present legislation permits should not be granted to persons who want to buy and occupy land in Villa Liza, Don Park and Windmill Park. I did not mention Apex but it is within 2 km of Actonville. The Benoni complex is going to grow and the people there need more residential land. The people from Natal are migrating to the Transvaal because that is where more and more jobs are being created, and those people also need houses. Similarly the people in and around Germiston need land. I see no reason whatsoever why these four areas should not be allowed for development. Only traitors to the whole of South Africa will say that one should only allow one area to be developed while the other areas are being held up. This is because it is not in the interest of the totality of our people to have any kind of Group Areas. It is the Group Areas Act that creates these artificial divisions and the unnecessary tensions among our people.</p>

<p>There has unfortunately been an abuse of the procedures available to hon members of this House. An example of this in the last couple of days was the long and rambling notice of motion, the total purport of which was to make a serious and false allegation against me. The allegation made by the hon member for Lenasia South, I think it was, is a completely false allegation in that he said that someone else had alleged that I had committed perjury. Stuff and nonsense! Unfortunately he was allowed to leave it and he was not obliged to retract that.</p>

<p>It was yesterday, or the day before, that we had this strange and rambling notice of motion proposed by an hon Cabinet Minister which also sought, in a motion, to make indictments. And what happens now? Those two motions were introduced in this House&#x2014;they did not appear in the disgraceful form in which they were framed in the Order Papers, they were cleaned up for the purpose of the Order Papers&#x2014;and were then telexed to the newspapers. And I have been approached by newspaper correspondents from various parts of the country to comment on the telexes which they say came to them from the hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

<p>I do not know as I have not seen the telexes, but if it so, it is also a further abuse of state amenities. A telex machine in the office of the hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, or in the Administration of the House of Delegates, does not exist for this kind of rubbish. It is there for the work which benefits the public, for the work of the Government to be done. The staff employed by the House of Delegates administration are not there for this kind of nonsensical political work on behalf of a politician or a political party. Yet those people have the temerity to come and try to lecture to us.</p>

<p>Fortunately I still have a little time and I would just like to deal more fully with the question of donations. Kistasamy Naidoo claims in a sworn affidavit that he was granted land by the hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council&#x2014;or at his instance&#x2014;for purposes of development in order to make a profit for himself and that he was required to make a donation to the NPP. He says that he donated a sum of R5 000.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Required by whom?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>He also says in his sworn affidavit that a firm called SSS Construction &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>You have most probably invented that now.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, that stupid remark I shall ignore.</p>

<p>On page 5, paragraph 8. 1 of his affidavit dated 3 October 1987, Mr Kistasamy Naidoo states:</p>

<block name="quote">Dashanya Residential Development which donated a sum of R5 20000,00 to the National Peoples Party of which the leader was and is Mr Amichand Rajbansi who is the person who stipulated that those receiving allocations were expected to make donations to his party. I confirm that the sum of R5 20000,00 paid by me to Mr Rajbansi&#x2019;s Party was not done out of a desire to support that Party but because I was expected to do so.</block>

<p>I submit that if what Mr Kistasamy Naidoo said is correct then it is a serious contravention of the Prevention of Corruption Act.</p>

<p>A firm called SSS Construction which had also been awarded State assets, later also made a payment of R5 000. The actual payment, I am informed, was made by SSS Construction to Mr <span class="col_399-400" refersTo="page_0222"/>Yusuf Seedat, a member of Parliament nominated thereto at the instance of the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and/or his party. I do not suggest that Mr Seedat received any personal benefit therefrom.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Blame the chairman!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>What I do say is that an advantage was gained by the hon the Chairman of the Minister&#x2019;s Council and his party from the allocation of State assets to SSS Construction. Mr Seedat, MP, will no doubt be able to explain what he did with the money.</p>

<p>Then Mr Kistasamy Naidoo goes on. Golden City Housing is, so it has been claimed, a public utility company interested in providing housing for the public on a non-profit basis. What one understands is that such a company will not permit its directors or shareholders to make any profit which should, on a proper construction, preclude not only dividends but also salaries or remuneration for acting as director other than of course arising out of actual employment and work actually done.</p>

<p>It follows that it is contrary to the principles on which public utility companies are granted special exemptions both in regard to the Companies Act and even in tax matters for such a company to make donations or to pay for the costs of a holding of a congress of a political party. I realise that as to such a company per se which conducts itself contrary to such principles, the Advocate General may not have jurisdiction. However the facts involved are germane, since they reflect upon advantage gained whether directly or indirectly by a Minister of State as a consequence of the allocation of State assets to such a company.</p>

<p>Golden City Housing purports to be an association not for gain although in 1985 it sought and was, under the direct or indirect influence or approval of the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, allocated about 150 pieces of land, being State properties.</p>

<p>As I have pointed out in my memorandum, Golden City Housing has made a donation of R20 000 for sports sponsorship. If that seems quite extraordinary, what is astounding is that the said Golden City Housing paid more than R7 000, being the costs of the holding of a congress of the NPP of which the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is the leader. When this aspect was raised in the House of Delegates, Mr Rajbansi professed ignorance of who paid those costs. This was of course very surprising and quite incredible as it is well known that nothing of significance happens in this party without Mr Rajbansi&#x2019;s knowledge or consent. In any event, he was made aware of the facts relating to the matter and the fact that, as appears below, Golden City Housing has once again now in 1987 been a principal beneficiary in the allotment of land in Lenasia establishes clearly a relationship of mutual advantage between Golden City Housing and Mr Rajbansi.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I will continue if you want some more.</p>

<p>Golden City Housing Association was apparently incorporated on 24 September 1985 and as will appear from annexure &#x201C;A&#x201D; hereto, its directors are: Mohamed Haneef Mia and Sayed Hoosen Mia, both of whom give the same addresses, namely 7006 Seal Crescent, Extension 7, Lenasia and 156 Grand Place, Lenasia.</p>

<p>Then he goes on &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>A company named SA Reza Finance (Pty) Limited was apparently formed on 11 April 1986 having a Mr Liyakatally Mohamed as director who gives as his business address 156 Grand Place, Lenasia and postal address as P O Box 492, Lenasia which is exactly the same as the Mia brothers give. It is noteworthy that Reza was one of the recipients of Mr Rajbansi&#x2019;s favour in respect of the allocation to it of State property both in 1985-86 &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I regret to inform the hon member that his time has expired.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, if anyone in this House is interested in the truth, in clean administration, he will be careful about the company he keeps.</p>

<p>This is so because I was reading the ethics of journalism, and as an attorney I expect the hon member for Reservoir Hills to know that there is a phrase in legal circles: <i>Audi alteram partem.</i> There is a very good reason why Parliament passed a law in connection with the production&#x2026; <span class="col_401-402" refersTo="page_0223"/>[Interjections.] No, I shall not take any questions now. However, I shall definitely take the question at the end of my speech.</p>

<p>That is also a reason why Parliament passed a law giving members of Parliament certain protection. Journalists and even others are very careful about the effect of what they finally do when there are, wittingly or unwittingly, omissions, or where one deliberately presents a one-sided picture of things.</p>

<p>This afternoon the hon member for Reservoir Hills, with his usual circumlocution took approximately 40 minutes to come to the topic. One thing has become a joke since yesterday. The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition attacked the hon member Mr S Abram and held me responsible because he said that he belongs to my party. I do not allocate land, but I have letters from former Ministers of Community Development confirming people who are resettlement cases. Nobody can tell me that they did not deal with people who came to their homes or their offices for assistance. I receive representations in my capacity as Chairman of the Executive Committee of the SAIC. I am aware that my predecessor, who is now the Leader of the Official Opposition, also received representations. However, if I received the direct representations, I am at fault. On the other hand, if others in the Ministers&#x2019; Council did certain things, blame the Chairman. Blame the leader. &#x201C;Blame the Chairman&#x201D; has been a catch phrase for everything in this House since yesterday.</p>

<p>Society passes judgment on the company one keeps. I am a Minister of State. Now I am a Minister of Housing. If I bow down to threats, then I am not fit to hold my positions. No mobster from the Transvaal, no strong-arm men will control my work and my Ministry.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">Hon MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Hear, hear!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>If they have succeeded with others &#x2026; I am a former football referee, and I ask each budding politician to spend five years in football administration in South Africa. If you refereed Orlando Pirates when Eric &#x201C;Scara&#x201D; Sono and Mainline Xhosa were alive, and they lost the match in Natalspruit grounds, then you have passed your test. Therefore, dealing with the hon member for Reservoir Hills is child&#x2019;s play when one had to deal with Eric Scara Sono&#x2014;a great player. You could not referee an Orlando Pirates match, especially in Natalspruit Grounds, and walk out of the football ground alive. I was one of the few who did.</p>

<p>Therefore there is a good reason, and the hon member for Reservoir Hills knows the reason for my saying this. Society passes judgment on the company one keeps.</p>

<p>There are people who come into my office. They ask for an appointment, and I cannot prejudge what they are going to discuss. They sometimes get the boot out of my office. When they get the boot out of my office I know who they had supper with that night. There are some people who joined us&#x2014;and even joined others&#x2014;for reasons of sincerity. Some join us for favours, and I believe in one thing.</p>

<p>I believe in the principle of no favours asked, no favours given. Yet I assist people on compassionate grounds. The other day I received a request which was against our rules. Some people&#x2019;s homes were collapsing outside the boundary of the city of Durban. I assisted them with homes overnight. Still, others will point a finger, saying that I made this allocation. However, I merely asked my department whether these people could be treated on compassionate grounds. Had the hon member for Reservoir Hills been honest today, I could say that I will take his pants off before I die. [Interjections.] He will never take my pants off. Call it what you like, we are living in Africa. In African terms we can call it &#x2018;tauza&#x2019;. [Interjections.] In this House I stated that I would be the first to request the hon the State President to appoint a judicial commission of enquiry. One cannot go to the hon the State President and play the fool. He is running a country, so that I must have reasonable grounds for my requests when I go to him.</p>

<p>I must comment on the manner in which some hon members are carrying on. Because they failed to beat us in the political arena they are now getting personal. On Sunday night I received a threat. Certain hitmen would get me if I did not bow down to a Transvaler&#x2014;not an MP&#x2014;and also if I continue to embarrass a certain member of this House. I do not fall for threats and if it was the intention of someone to control contractors and all land allocations in Lenasia, that intention has disappeared into ashes as long as I head the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

<p><span class="col_403-404" refersTo="page_0224"/>The hon member for Reservoir Hills stood up at one of the sessions of this House and talked about Lonhro, etc. God was on our side. A telex arrived, saying that Lonhro was one of those companies which at that stage had failed to commence the construction of the homes.</p>

<p>I would like to make a statement today. In the past 10 years accusations have been levelled against me, yet I have survived. I survived because truth was on my side. When the lid is off, the hon member for Reservoir Hills&#x2019; name will be associated with each one of these accusations. I do not run away. I have taken this fight into the arena of the hon member for Reservoir Hills. Only a guilty man runs away. I want the hon members of this House to know that ever since that shameful day in 1985 when an accusation was made, that accusation has affected my wife and I. [Interjections.] I said allegations. On the 25th February 1985 the hon member for Reservoir Hills said and I quote:</p>

<block name="quote">I will repeat these statements outside this House and the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council can take up the matter in an arena where he can put questions to me in cross-examination.</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I did! In Durban, in court, in public.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member for Reservoir Hills says that he did. I will prove to this House that he substituted the name &#x2018;Rajbansi&#x2019; with the word &#x2018;politician&#x2019;.</p>

<p>He deliberately substituted that. Why did he not use the name Rajbansi in court? What is the hon member for Reservoir Hills&#x2019; opinion? Is he not protected in court? He made certain statements under protection in court. I said I was going to sue the hon member for Reservoir Hills. The hon member for Reservoir Hills knows that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Your name is mentioned several times.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>The name is mentioned when the prosecutor asks questions. The name is mentioned in sentences where I cannot sue you for defamation. However, he did not repeat those allegations in court. I want to deal with that court case because we have a notice of motion. I am not going to deal with it fully today but it should be remembered that if I had an allegation to make, I would not say that someone unknown to me came into my office. [Interjections.] He said in the <i>Tribune Herald:</i> &#x201C;I have the evidence&#x201D;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Tell us about Jeevan Seebran. Is he not your front man?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I am coming to that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Tell us about Tony Adams.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I want it noted that the hon member for Reservoir Hills has said: &#x201C;Isn&#x2019;t he your front man?&#x201D;. I would like that to be recorded.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, I asked you a question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I would like that to be recorded. One cannot make insinuations by way of questions or interjections.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>What about the butcher&#x2019;s licence?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>He then went on to say on 28 January 1985: &#x201C;I will repeat it outside the House&#x201D;. He is interjecting. I have waited for this day.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Did you not write out Seebran&#x2019;s application?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I have waited for this day. No amount of interjections &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: May I respectfully request that you ask the hon member for Reservoir Hills to restrain himself?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council may continue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I want the hon member for Reservoir Hills to know that when a man stands up in this House and makes serious allegations, the person who stands accused does not take it lightly. It has hurt me. It has affected me. It has affected my wife and children.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from><span class="col_405-406" refersTo="page_0225"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>I am sorry about that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Stop interfering with my family! [Interjections.] I want to make that appeal. Stop interfering with my family!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>No questions. I will come back to Jeevan Seebran. I will come back to everyone.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, if anyone has interfered with the hon the Minister&#x2019;s family that is a serious state of affairs and it should be reported to the Police.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>It should be reported to the Police. Yes, if anyone has serious allegations let them walk out with me at 6. 30 p m this evening and we will walk across to the police station.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>You made a stupid allegation just now.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You are a wounded animal in a cage&#x2014;no, not in a cage, in a post box. [Interjections.] Furthermore, the hon member is walking on the crutches of an informant. He is walking on the crutches of Kessa Thambi.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Kessa is not afraid.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Ask the Transvaal who Kessa Thambi is, what his history is and what his record is.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>But he is not afraid.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I would not stand up here and use the word &#x201C;informant&#x201D; but I am going to say something this afternoon. I am going to say that an hon member of this House told me a year ago and again a week ago that the hon member for Reservoir Hills had informed him that he had made a mistake in respect of those allegations. The hon member for Reservoir Hills then apparently asked him to ask me not to pursue this matter.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Whoever said that is a liar.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I do not have the permission of the hon member concerned to divulge his name, but I am making this statement here under oath.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Well, if he tells lies, that is all right by him.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>What if the informant tells lies? I want the hon member for Reservoir Hills to know that I am going to say what I planned to say this afternoon. His interjections will not dissuade me from doing so.</p>

<p>On 21 February 1985 the hon member for Reservoir Hills said the following (Hansard: Delegates, 21 February 1985, col 812):</p>

<block name="quote">We may even go to the extent of making these charges outside the precincts of this House so that the person who is accused of maladministration, bribery and corruption, can avail himself of the redress which is available to him at common law.</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Why did you sue Kista Sami?</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, Mr Kessa Thambi has made a statement to the advocate-general privily.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>No, I am talking about the <i>Sunday Tribune.</i></p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I will answer the hon member.
